# Evidence index

Read this first. One row per piece of evidence: what it shows, its numbers, where it ran, whether
its proofs were real, how to re-check it, and the file with the details (every number below is
taken from that file, or from the suite run of this commit for the last section). Words used for
where it ran: **in-process harness** (LEZ v0.4.0-rc1's own state machine and a copy of its fee
settlement, no sequencer), **live rc1 sequencer** (LEZ's unmodified v0.4.0-rc1 sequencer with a
Logos Blockchain node), **live rc2 sequencer** (the same at v0.4.0-rc2), **local EVM** (Foundry), **fork of Sepolia** (anvil), **Sepolia** (read-only
calls, except the transactions of rows 26, 41, 91, 142 and 154), **one L4 GPU** (one NVIDIA L4 on Modal), **build box** (a
Modal CPU container), **laptop CPU**, **simulator**, **public LEZ testnet** (LEZ v0.3.1 at `testnet.lez.logos.co`, row
150). **Stand-in** marks data we made ourselves where real data would go. Rows are numbered in the order they were
added. Nothing here is audited. **Since 21 November 2026:** every proof in this index is made with RISC Zero 3.0.5,
whose recursion and Groth16 circuits accept forged claims (GHSA-g6qg-9j8p-7j87, GHSA-mm6c-35cm-g4x2, 11 November
2026): a recorded seal or receipt was made by our provers from its recorded inputs, but its acceptance by a 3.0.x
verifier, on chain or off, shows only that it is well-formed ([security note](security/risc0-groth16-advisory-2026-11-21/README.md)). Every transaction sent to a public network is on Sepolia, from the owner's runs (the
one exception, 11 transactions on the public LEZ testnet, is row 150): the first attempt's one transaction, which ran
out of gas, then the second run's deployment of the v3 vault and its deposit 0 (row 26), which a LEZ sequencer we run
then claimed (row 27), and the round trip of the v5 vault, live on 11 November 2026: its deployment, two deposits, a
checkpoint and two releases, 13 transactions (row 41); and the same round trip on the final programs, live on 12
November 2026, 13 transactions more (row 91); and with the LEZ side on LEZ v0.4.0-rc3, live on 18 November 2026, 13
more (row 142); and the owner's eight `setGlobalCap` transactions of 21 November 2026 (the deployer's nonces 49–56),
which closed the four vaults (row 154). On a public LEZ network only the light client's experimental v0.3.1 port is
deployed (row 150); none of the proposal's v0.4 programs is: the round trip's LEZ side is a devnet we ran (LEZ testnet
0.4 does not exist).

| 136 | **The malicious-tenant test (F.16) on the deployed bridge program: two known issues recorded** (found by an independent review of the v0.3.1 port, 18 November). R1: a second configuration of the same vault and chain, reading Ethereum through an attestation instance its creator controls (its records placed directly), pairs, imports a root with the vault's real leaves and claims the paired configuration's public-kind deposit with its known `s`; the paired configuration's claim of it is then refused (250): the deposit is frozen (no theft, no double mint: the second configuration's mint and burn stay under its own id; no refund once the paired configuration has imported it). A plaintext claim copied before inclusion and ordered first does the same; a private-kind deposit is refused to it (249 without `s`, 252 with it). R2: LEZ v0.4 has no state size limit, so the v0.3.1 port's 1,505-holding panic does not occur, but `holder(dest)`, shared by every configuration, can be filled (placed directly) until a claim to it exceeds the gas limit. ## What the evidence does not show yet

- **The round trip on a public LEZ network.** The whole round trip ran twice on Sepolia (each time the v5 vault, a
  public and a private deposit, both claimed on LEZ, a public and a private burn, a rule (B) checkpoint and both
  releases): on `integration5`'s programs (row 41, after three rehearsals on forks, row 31) and on the final programs
  (row 91, after the fork rehearsals of row 83). Its LEZ side is a devnet we ran: one
  validator, one channel key (n = t = 1), leader proofs in dev mode, the attestation signed by the run's driver; no
  public LEZ v0.4 network exists (LEZ testnet 0.4 does not; the public LEZ testnet runs v0.3.1, where only the light
  client's experimental port is deployed, row 150). The vault there has testnet parameters (a 1-day revocation window,
  a 120 s release delay, no caps, no watchers, a freeze authority without a key holder), not the proposal's production
  values, and one throwaway account was depositor, recipient and prover.
- **The bridge program on a sequencer** has run only on devnets we ran (LEZ v0.4.0-rc1, one validator, dev-mode
  leader proofs: the crown devnets, the four- and five-sequencer zone of rows 100–101, the devnet of rows 102–103),
  where its private claim and private burn were proven for real: the deployed program's build (`0xccde6171…71dd`)
  in the fork rehearsals of row 83, the live run of row 91, the zone of row 100 and the devnet of row 103, `integration5`'s (`0x0e9dcd79…bbb6` at the crown build path,
  [`e2e/crown/README.md`](../e2e/crown/README.md)) in rows 31 and 41. Its other
  figures (rows 8, 9, 28 and 30) are from the in-process harness in dev mode; row 10's GPU proofs are of the earlier
  `25dfd9b2…`.
- **Tenant isolation on the deployed bridge program.** The malicious-tenant test (row 136) shows that the configurations are not strictly separated: a second configuration of the same vault and chain can spend the nullifier of a public-kind deposit whose `s` it knows, freezing that deposit for the paired configuration (no theft, no double mint; once the paired configuration has imported the deposit, no refund either), and a destination's holder account, shared by every configuration, can be filled until claims to it exceed LEZ's gas limit. The fix (nullifier and holder accounts keyed by the configuration) changes the bridge program's image id, so deploying it is milestone B1's; it is built in the LEZ v0.3.1 port and, for v0.4, in a second build of the bridge program that is not deployed (row 143), which no vault pins ([`bridge.md`](design/bridge.md) §3, "Known issues, 18 November 2026").
- **The final checkpoint and release images were proven only on devnets we ran**: on the four-sequencer zone
  (rows 100, 101: n = 4, t = 3 and n = 5, t = 4, into a v5 vault on a local anvil); in the fork rehearsals of row 83
  (`crown-rehearsal-4` and `-5`) and the live run of row 91 (its seals verified on Sepolia): `checkpoint_b` over each
  devnet's own blocks, n = t = 1 (blocks 25..=90 in row 91), and both releases of each. crown-live-1's (the
  same sources, built at the maintainer's path) were proven in the rehearsal of row 31 and the live run of row 41:
  `checkpoint_b` over devnet blocks 19..=95 and 179..=252, and both releases of each. Rule (A)'s current checkpoint image has no proof. Rows
  15–18 and 24 (the real-block proof included) are of images from before the `ImportRoot` fix; the fixed checkpoint
  refuses the return leg's import (row 18), and re-making that fixture is not done. The vault's real fixtures
  (`vault/test/real/`) are of those earlier images, but for `zone-bridge.json` and `zone-bridge-rotation.json`
  (rows 100, 101: 10 tests), which are of the final ones.
- **Mainnet-depth state for the bridge.** The deployed bridge program ran its imports and registrations under
  synthetic state tries of about 40 accounts (row 9) and on Sepolia's real state in the live run of row 91 (`Init`,
  Register ETH, `ImportFees` and two `ImportRoot`s against the v5 vault, 359,299 to 577,165 execution gas;
  `integration5`'s program the same in row 41, 359,397 to 577,927; the frozen program's `Init` and `ImportRoot` in row
  27: 355,935 and 426,479); a mainnet-depth attestation was measured for the
  attestation program (row 3: 274,842 cycles for one slot), not for the bridge's import.
- **On the light-client side**, the program's real proofs on a sequencer are row 6's (an image from before the
  attestation existed) and row 123's (the final programs' private update and a private attestation, on LEZ
  v0.4.0-rc3 with one local validator); the current source has otherwise run on sequencers without proofs in the fork rehearsals of row 83
  and in the live run of row 91 (`Init` from a real Sepolia checkpoint inside its weak-subjectivity bound, which
  refused a 38-day-old one), earlier sources in rows 21, 22, 31 and 41.
- **The checkpoints over real blocks** are of one-validator devnets with one channel key (n = t = 1), but one: row 24's
  chain has no bridge program, and its attestation was signed after the fact from a copy of the node's state; rows
  31, 41 and 91 carry the bridge program and both burns, and their attestations were signed by the run's driver once the
  tip was final on the node. Rows 100 and 101 are of a zone of four sequencers (row 65's) carrying the bridge program and
  both burns, attested by an attestor beside each sequencer (3 of 4 collected; in row 101 after a rotation its
  committee proposed and Bedrock applied, 4 of the 5 keys), proven and accepted by a v5 vault on a local anvil, both
  burns released; their beacon chain on LEZ and their Ethereum side are stand-ins. `ferrata_attestor_module` has run beside each sequencer of a second run of row 65's zone
  (four Logos Cores, row 95): that zone has no bridge program, and its 3-of-4 input was checked by the rule
  natively, not proven.
- **Not measured**: user-side proving on a laptop (rows 32–34 and 38 are cloud servers held to 8 and to 16 CPUs with
  16 GiB, row 88 two Mac minis under macOS; at 8 CPUs the attestation and the claim take about 15 minutes, 19–21
  padded, 14 on an M1), and on a GPU under macOS (r0vm 3.0.5 has no Metal prover).
- **Image ids**: since `integration7` guest builds are independent of the build path (row 82: 14 of 14 guests
  byte-identical at two paths with two `$HOME`s); the first live run's programs (row 37) reproduce only at the
  maintainer's checkout path; the final programs, live in row 91, rebuild to that vault's ids (row 92). The Dockerfile
  that pins the toolchains was built and run on Modal and, with `scripts/reproduce/run.sh`, under Docker on a fresh
  x86-64 machine (row 90).
- **Privacy** (row 23) is measured in dev mode with a mock verifier on synthetic records, and again on real proofs
  of the final programs (row 89: every claim, burn and release proven, the releases checked by RISC Zero's Groth16
  verifier in the vault); the burn root's checkpoint is still the mock there (the harness's blocks are not chained,
  row 89). Timing and amount residuals, network metadata and LEZ's own anonymity set are outside both.
- **Not built** (README, "Specified but not built"): among them the A3 gate's three daemons on a running sequencer
  (the three daemons are built as Logos modules, rows 94–98; the updater module carries both backends and followed
  Sepolia on a crown zone inside a headless Logos Core, row 104; the binary followed it for eight hours, row 99),
  the monitor's client of the LEZ and Logos Blockchain nodes' RPCs, sends through Logos Core's own modules and
  verified reads beyond the attestation module's (the attestation and bridge modules' Ethereum reads go through
  `eth_rpc_module`, rows 108–110; the attestation module's through `verified_proxy_module` on public Sepolia, the
  updater's light-client data through `beacon_api_module`, rows 116–118; on a
  LEZ sequencer the user's private round trip has run through the tool and through the mini-app and
  `ferrata_bridge_module` in a headless Logos Core, with a stand-in for those modules, rows 102 and 103; the bridge module's LEZ reads and private claim and burn go
  through Logos Core's `lez_core`, patched, rows 113–114; the mini-app's private round trip has run inside Logos
  Basecamp with its Ethereum side through the stand-in, row 125, and with its private claim and burn proven for real
  there, row 126: Basecamp still cuts a view's call to a core module at 20 s, and the module runs the two as jobs the
  view polls), a published module catalog. Nothing is audited: the static
  analysis, symbolic checks, fuzzing and the core's review (rows 1, 35, 36 and 40) are tooling, not an audit; the
  Halmos properties hold from one concrete fixture state within stated bounds; of the four not proven in 900 s,
  one passed with 4,800 s in its generic layout and three did not finish
  ([`release-engineering.md`](release-engineering.md)).
- **LEZ v0.4.0-rc3 and Logos Blockchain 0.3.5-rc.1** (rows 111, 112, 123, 124, 129, 130, 141, 142): the final programs ran on
  rc3's own sequencer and node, and the return leg over such a zone runs with a second build of the two checkpoint
  guests, which reads the transactions' fork digest (rows 129, 130), but with one sequencer, one local validator and
  the vault on a local anvil, a fork of Sepolia (row 141) or Sepolia itself (`crown-live-4`, row 142: a new vault
  pinning the second build's ids, since the earlier vaults' checkpoint ids read the `d480efc6` layout and cannot follow
  such a zone): not on Logos's devnet, not with several sequencers, and
  not across a Bedrock era change, after which that build refuses the new digest
  ([`lez/results/v0.4.0-rc3/return-leg/`](../lez/results/v0.4.0-rc3/return-leg/README.md)); the four real proofs of M0 check 4
  ran on rc3 with one local validator (row 123), not on Logos's devnet, and on that node the sequencer once orphaned
  120 unmined inscriptions after 20 minutes and rewound its head (logged, not traced in the node; again during row 124's
  session); no real proof has been made on rc2.
- **The gas drop** (row 124): one drop and one claim on one local validator, so no anonymity set; the relayer's check
  ran offline (`ferrata-gasdrop relay`, the zone's roots and claimed nullifiers handed over as files), not in the daemon
  `ferrata-relayer`, whose credit is still a public transfer that no private claim can sweep; the relayer's funds were
  LEZ's test private account; burns with a drop are not shown.

## The latest additions, of 11 to 14 November 2026

Moved here from the README's Evidence section on 12 November 2026, unchanged (only the links are rewritten for
this folder); the README keeps one line for each. The live run on the final programs was added here the same
evening, the five Logos modules on one tree on 13 November, the updater on a running sequencer the same day,
the build's last merges after them, and LEZ v0.4.0-rc3 and Logos Core's `lez_core` on 14 November.

**Merged on 14 November 2026** (EVIDENCE rows 111–115; the branches `lez-core-v04` and `lez-rc3`): Logos Core's own
LEZ module, `lez_core`, at its merged v0.4 state (`cc73ebf`), built from its source and with our patch, loaded in a
headless Logos Core beside `ferrata_bridge_module` with the user's LEZ wallet opened inside it: on a LEZ v0.4.0-rc1
devnet the bridge's state reads through it equal to the plain path's (85 of 85 actor-state reads, 9 of 9
private-holding reads), and the user's private claim and burn executed and proven by LEZ's wallet inside Logos Core,
with real proofs in 248.7 s and 228.8 s, verified by the sequencer and applied in LEZ blocks 74 and 98 (rows 113,
114); what Logos Core's modules cannot do for us yet, measured: as merged, `lez_core` refuses a program's PDA as an
account and mentions no account authorizing, so the bridge program refuses the claim (the patch fixes both), and its
block and transaction queries answer only for a sequencer it runs in-process; `eth_rpc_module` 6965f32 needs a 6-line glue
patch to compile with the builder's own locks (row 115; with it, rows 119-121: the monitor's and relayer's Ethereum
transactions through `tx_sender_module` on anvil, 7 of 7 equal to the plain path); LEZ v0.4.0-rc3: the programs rebuilt from
unchanged sources (every image id moves, the code does not; 1,119 of 1,120 harness table rows equal to rc1's, the
other an image-derived identifier) and the final programs on rc3's own sequencer and Logos Blockchain node
0.3.5-rc.1 (182 blocks; 99 sent, 76 applied, 19 refused with their codes; 64 of the 65 with a harness row at the
harness's execution gas); checkpoint-core refuses 0.3.5-rc.1's fork-digest framing, so the checkpoint guests must
change for Logos's devnet (rows 111, 112). Checked together on the tree to publish (`final-verify`, dev mode): the
native suites, `check.sh` with the six modules, 0 step and 0 check failures, and the one-command check at `a87cebe`,
5 of 5 PASS (row 90; [`modules/results/final-verify-2026-11-14/`](../modules/results/final-verify-2026-11-14/README.md)).

**Merged on 13 November 2026** (EVIDENCE rows 100–110; the branches `zone-bridge`, `updater-module-seq`,
`relayer-module`, `logos-core-io` and `miniapp-sequencer`): the bridge program on a zone of four LEZ sequencers with an
attestor beside each, its rule (B) checkpoint over the zone's real blocks proven into FerrataVault v5 on a local
anvil, and a key rotation the zone's committee proposed carried into the checkpoint (rows 100, 101); the user's
private round trip on a running LEZ sequencer and node, through the `ferrata` tool's sequencer path and through the
mini-app driving `ferrata_bridge_module` in a headless Logos Core, the private claim and burn proven for real and
verified by the sequencer in the mini-app's run (rows 102, 103); `ferrata_updater_module` with its backend for a
running LEZ sequencer, in a headless Logos Core on a crown zone following Sepolia, and `verify_checkpoint`'s provider
checks (rows 104, 105); the relayer's root imports and `ferrata_relayer_module` (rows 106, 107); the attestation and
bridge modules' Ethereum reads through Logos Core's `eth_rpc_module`, and what Logos Core's other modules cannot do
yet (rows 108–110). Checked together on the merged tree (`final-merge`, dev mode): `cli/`'s tests, its e2e and the
relayer's suite (the fork's gas figures equal to row 46's), the updater's tests, `monitor/` and
`checkpoint/attestor`; `check.sh` with the six modules, dev and portable, 0 step and 0 check failures (53 calls equal
to native in both Logos Cores, every job of the four daemons); `logos-io.sh` with every read through
`eth_rpc_module` equal to the plain one ([`modules/results/final-2026-11-13/`](../modules/results/final-2026-11-13/README.md)).

**Live on 12–13 November 2026, merged on 13 November** (EVIDENCE rows 99 and 80; the branch `updater-live`): the
updater daemon with its backend for a running LEZ sequencer (`lez/updater/sequencer/`) kept the crown run's Sepolia
instance following Sepolia live for about eight hours on LEZ v0.4.0-rc1 devnets, from Lodestar's light-client data
with each finalized header confirmed canonical by publicnode: 74 updates applied end to end (224 transactions, none
refused; 56 updates and 170 transactions on the chains kept, 18 and 54 on blocks a preempted container lost), across the sync-committee period boundary 1411 → 1412 (the committees rotated, period 1413's adopted),
restarts mid-job and of the whole zone ([`lez/updater/results/live-sepolia/`](../lez/updater/results/live-sepolia/README.md)).
Merged into the daemon the updater module drives, with the planning in `lez/advance`: the updater's 14 tests, the
A3 gate's eight runs (every figure the same), the module's job in both Logos Cores and the five modules' check,
step and check failures 0; the records re-checked, ALL PASS
([`lez/updater/results/merged-2026-11-13/`](../lez/updater/results/merged-2026-11-13/README.md)).

**Merged on 13 November 2026** (EVIDENCE rows 93–98; the branches `bridge-module-complete` and `modules-more`): the
five Logos modules on one tree, built by logos-module-builder and loaded together in a headless Logos Core, dev and
portable: `ferrata_attest_module` with `advance`, and `ferrata_bridge_module` with the private claim, `import_root`,
registry reads, the release rule and `prove_release`, on the tools' own code (`cli/wallet`, `lez/advance`; one
release proven for real through Logos Core); and the three daemons as modules, `ferrata_attestor_module` (also
beside each sequencer of a running zone of four), `ferrata_monitor_module` (its anvil rehearsal, 46 of 46) and
`ferrata_updater_module` (mainnet and Sepolia caught up on the in-process LEZ chain); every job through the modules,
also installed from the local catalog; step and check failures 0 ([`modules/RESULTS.md`](../modules/RESULTS.md)).

**Live on 12 November 2026** (EVIDENCE rows 91–92, `crown-live-3`): the round trip on the final programs on Sepolia,
Ethereum to LEZ and back, through FerrataVault v5 `0x04eE8FF19cC11CEE4aE65d03e12b9888043de44c`: two deposits, both
claimed on LEZ (the private one with a real proof), two burns, the `checkpoint_b` proof over the devnet's blocks on a
GPU, its seal and both release seals verified on Sepolia, both releases paid; 19 of 19 stages, verify 14 of 14
([`e2e/runs/crown-live-3/`](../e2e/runs/crown-live-3/README.md)). LEZ's light client started from a fresh Sepolia
checkpoint, after the programs' weak-subjectivity bound refused the 38-day-old one of `crown-live-2`, which sent
nothing ([`e2e/runs/crown-live-2/`](../e2e/runs/crown-live-2/README.md)). Anyone's check from the committed records:
13 passed, 0 failed on two public endpoints; this tree's guests rebuilt: 6 of 6 crown programs and the vault's two
immutables equal.

**Merged on 12 November 2026** (EVIDENCE rows 84–88; the four branches of `integration8`): the `ferrata` tool
complete for a user, private by default (deposit to a private account, the claim held by its delay and proven
privately with the wallet's own keys, a private burn, the release through any relayer), every step a dry run that
prints what becomes public, with recovery from the seed alone and 23 refusals and holds named before signing
([`cli/README.md`](../cli/README.md)); the mini-app's private round trip on its defaults settled on its devnet,
also with the private claim and burn proven for real, 242.4 s and 209.4 s on a CPU
([`modules/ferrata_bridge_ui/RESULTS.md`](../modules/ferrata_bridge_ui/RESULTS.md)); the padded private attestation,
claim and burn proven on Apple Silicon under macOS, 14.3 minutes at most on an M1 Mac mini with 16 GB and
7.9 on an M4, on the CPU (r0vm 3.0.5 has no Metal prover)
([`lez/results/p4-macos/README.md`](../lez/results/p4-macos/README.md)).

**Since 11 November 2026** (EVIDENCE rows 28-30): the bridge program's freezes and caps run on time it reads from
its own attestation program's records, never on a caller's time ([`lez/BRIDGE.md`](../lez/BRIDGE.md) §6); rule (B)
also checks the tip's producer inscription, so a forged history needs one channel key besides `t` attestation keys
([`checkpoint/RESULTS.md`](../checkpoint/RESULTS.md) §4a); and the findings of the security review of 11 November
([`docs/reviews/security/review-2026-11-11.md`](reviews/security/review-2026-11-11.md)) are fixed on both sides (LEZ: public balances at `holder(account)` PDAs, `ImportRoot` only as a visible
call, one `NOut` per burn, a MAC over the memo; vault: the keyless-retirement clock, the checkpoint fee to the
prover, stale candidates, no deposit after a final sunset, test chains only, a bound on `revokeWindow`), with tests
that fail on the code before the fixes ([`lez/BRIDGE-RESULTS.md`](../lez/BRIDGE-RESULTS.md) §13,
[`vault/GAS.md`](../vault/GAS.md)). These changed the bridge, `checkpoint_b` and release images: the real crossing of
row 27 and the real proofs before it used the earlier programs; the crown run uses these, rehearsed on a fork of
Sepolia with a LEZ devnet and real proofs (row 31, [`e2e/crown/README.md`](../e2e/crown/README.md)) and then run live on
Sepolia to DONE on 11 November (row 41).

**Merged on 11 November 2026** (EVIDENCE rows 43–75; the ten branches of `integration7`): besides the rows
above, the weak-subjectivity bound in the core and the program, and successors with the root id (`Succeed`/`Spawn`:
a fork not in the schedule fixed at `Init` crossed by a successor under the parent's root id;
[`lez/results/a1-successors-ws.md`](../lez/results/a1-successors-ws.md)); the vault's deposit leaves on real Sepolia data
through the attestation program's leaf requests, and the reference consumer's rules 5 to 7 with public and private
claims of the vault's real deposits, proven for real on a CPU ([`lez/RESULTS.md`](../lez/RESULTS.md) §6); the bridge
program's B2 controls: the keyless `Sunset`, the freeze authority's 14-day handover, an M-of-N freeze authority,
watchers pausing LEZ for 12 hours and transfers between holders, the private transfer proven for real
([`lez/BRIDGE-RESULTS.md`](../lez/BRIDGE-RESULTS.md) §14); rule (B) with t of n attestors on a real four-sequencer zone
([`checkpoint/attestor/RESULTS.md`](../checkpoint/attestor/RESULTS.md)). The guests these changed have new image ids
([`docs/image-ids.md`](image-ids.md)); the rows of earlier runs keep the ids they ran.

**Added in the seventh pass** (11 November 2026, EVIDENCE rows 35–40): the vault through Slither, Aderyn and
Halmos and the Rust dependencies through cargo-audit and cargo-deny, every finding triaged: no defect in the vault,
7 Halmos properties proven and 4 not finished in their bound ([`docs/reviews/security/tools-2026-11-11.md`](reviews/security/tools-2026-11-11.md));
the crown run's six programs rebuilt to their image ids at the published crown build path
([`docs/guides/verify.md`](guides/verify.md); since `integration7` guest builds are independent of the path,

pads every private transaction and proven at 8 and 16 CPUs, 10.6 minutes at most at 16
([`lez/results/p4-minimum/README.md`](../lez/results/p4-minimum/README.md)); a second consumer of the attestation,
`ferrata_holdings`, which accepts an ERC-20 balance at a finalized block (consumer guide §12); seven fuzz targets
over the core, 1.79 billion executions, and seven more over the programs (the bridge program's decoding and
handler, the checkpoint's LEZ block parser differential against LEZ's own decoder, rule (B) on edits of the lab's
real input, the Mantle inscription parsers, the release and claim inputs and journals), 4.31 billion executions, no
fault found ([`fuzz/RESULTS.md`](../fuzz/RESULTS.md), "The programs"); and an independent review of the
core against the specifications, with 24 more mutants, all killed ([`docs/REVIEW-core.md`](REVIEW-core.md)).
